Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Run the Hermes agent inside the SWE-bench Docker container for code execution and testing.

PROBLEM:
Currently the Hermes agent runs on macOS (Python 3.11, warm imports), writes code, and tests it locally. Then SWE-bench scores in a Docker container (Python 3.9, clean state). Environment differences cause false passes: tests pass locally but fail in Docker. This accounts for multiple failures across our eval runs.

SOLUTION:
Make the executor phase run inside the same Docker container that SWE-bench uses for scoring. The agent reads files, edits code, runs tests — all inside the container. When tests pass in this environment, they'll pass in scoring too.

HOW SWE-BENCH DOCKER WORKS:
- SWE-bench builds Docker images per-repo: `sweb.env.py.x86_64.<hash>:latest`
- These images have the repo pre-installed at the right commit with all dependencies
- Tests run via `pytest` or the repo's test runner
- The `swebench.harness` module handles image building and test execution

WHAT WE NEED:
1. Before execute phase, build or pull the SWE-bench Docker image for the current task
2. Mount the workspace (with the code changes) into the container
3. Run the Hermes agent with the execute prompt, but with tool calls (terminal, file operations) executing INSIDE the container
4. The agent can read/write files in the mounted workspace and run tests in the container's environment
5. After execution, the patch is extracted from the container and scored normally

APPROACH OPTIONS (investigate and choose the best):

A. **Docker exec approach**: Build the SWE-bench image, start a container, mount the workspace. The hermes terminal tool runs commands via `docker exec` instead of local shell. File tools work on the mounted volume.

B. **Remote execution approach**: Start the container with an SSH server or API endpoint. Hermes connects to it for terminal commands. 

C. **Full agent in container**: Install hermes-agent inside the Docker container. Run the entire execute phase inside. Pass the prompt and API keys via env vars.

D. **Hybrid**: Run hermes locally but redirect ONLY test commands to Docker via a wrapper script.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must use the EXACT same Docker image SWE-bench uses for scoring
- Must not slow down the pipeline significantly (Docker image building can be cached)
- Must work with our existing eval infrastructure (run_evals.py, parallel workers)
- The agent needs API access (network) from inside the container for model calls
- File changes must persist and be extractable as a git diff

EXISTING CODE TO LEVERAGE:
- evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py — already has Docker image building logic for scoring
- The `swebench` Python package handles image lifecycle
- Our verify_after_execute step already runs tests in Docker

START BY:
1. Understanding how SWE-bench builds and runs Docker containers (read swebench.harness code)
2. Understanding how our verify step works (evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py verify_after_execute)
3. Prototyping the simplest approach that works
4. Testing with ONE task end-to-end

This is a significant infrastructure change. Get it working for a single task first, then we'll integrate it into the full pipeline.

    "Docker `--tmpfs` args are ADDITIVE, not last-wins. If _SECURITY_ARGS includes `--tmpfs /tmp:...` and extra_run_args also includes `--tmpfs /tmp:...`, Docker may error on duplicate mount points.   "meta_commentary": "**Key design decisions made during finalization:**\n\n1. **skip_security_args instead of extra_run_args override**: The plan proposed appending extra_run_args after _SECURITY_ARGS, but Docker --tmpfs mounts are additive (not last-wins), so duplicate /tmp mounts would error. Added `skip_security_args` flag that completely excludes _SECURITY_ARGS when True, letting extra_run_args provide replacement limits. This addresses flags correctness-2 and docker-sandbox-parity.\n\n2. **Prompt-based patch extraction with deterministic fallback**: The plan's patch extraction depends on the agent writing `git diff > /hermes_output/model_patch.diff`. This is acknowledged as agent-dependent (flag issue_hints-1). Mitigation: if model_patch.diff doesn't exist, score()/verify() fall back to host git diff, which returns empty (no changes on host) \u2192 scored as failed \u2192 triggers verify retry. The agent gets explicit \"REQUIRED\" language in the prompt. This is sufficient for v1.\n\n3. **Prebuild ordering fix**: Moved prebuild_docker_image() call between prepare_workspace() and read_prompt() in run_evals.py. This ensures the Docker image name is available when read_prompt() checks self._docker_execute_image to conditionally add Docker instructions. Addresses flags callers-1 and correctness-3.\n\n4. **Patch-only guard**: prebuild_docker_image() checks both swebench_docker_execute AND swebench_patch_only. Workers with patch_only=true skip prebuild entirely. Addresses flags plan-prebuild-parallel-cost, scope-2, callers-2.\n\n5. **Three container_config sites**: The executor MUST update all three: _get_env_config (primary), terminal_tool.py recreation path, and file_tools.py. Missing any one means a file_read or terminal recreation could create a Docker env without skip_home_overlay, breaking the conda environment.\n\n**Execution tips:**\n- T1 is trivial and unblocks T4's _strip_test_files_from_patch dependency\n- T2 and T3 are the Docker backend foundation \u2014 get these right and the rest is mostly wiring\n- For T4's _get_swebench_docker_image(), test the subprocess call manually first: `python -c \"from swebench.harness.docker_build import get_env_configs_to_build; print('API exists')\"`. If the API doesn't exist, implement fallback immediately.\n- T5 is the largest task \u2014 read the existing score() and verify_after_execute() carefully before modifying. The git diff logic must be preserved as the else-branch for non-Docker mode.",

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Run the Hermes agent inside the SWE-bench Docker container for code execution and testing.

PROBLEM:
Currently the Hermes agent runs on macOS (Python 3.11, warm imports), writes code, and tests it locally. Then SWE-bench scores in a Docker container (Python 3.9, clean state). Environment differences cause false passes: tests pass locally but fail in Docker. This accounts for multiple failures across our eval runs.

SOLUTION:
Make the executor phase run inside the same Docker container that SWE-bench uses for scoring. The agent reads files, edits code, runs tests — all inside the container. When tests pass in this environment, they'll pass in scoring too.

HOW SWE-BENCH DOCKER WORKS:
- SWE-bench builds Docker images per-repo: `sweb.env.py.x86_64.<hash>:latest`
- These images have the repo pre-installed at the right commit with all dependencies
- Tests run via `pytest` or the repo's test runner
- The `swebench.harness` module handles image building and test execution

WHAT WE NEED:
1. Before execute phase, build or pull the SWE-bench Docker image for the current task
2. Mount the workspace (with the code changes) into the container
3. Run the Hermes agent with the execute prompt, but with tool calls (terminal, file operations) executing INSIDE the container
4. The agent can read/write files in the mounted workspace and run tests in the container's environment
5. After execution, the patch is extracted from the container and scored normally

APPROACH OPTIONS (investigate and choose the best):

A. **Docker exec approach**: Build the SWE-bench image, start a container, mount the workspace. The hermes terminal tool runs commands via `docker exec` instead of local shell. File tools work on the mounted volume.

B. **Remote execution approach**: Start the container with an SSH server or API endpoint. Hermes connects to it for terminal commands. 

C. **Full agent in container**: Install hermes-agent inside the Docker container. Run the entire execute phase inside. Pass the prompt and API keys via env vars.

D. **Hybrid**: Run hermes locally but redirect ONLY test commands to Docker via a wrapper script.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must use the EXACT same Docker image SWE-bench uses for scoring
- Must not slow down the pipeline significantly (Docker image building can be cached)
- Must work with our existing eval infrastructure (run_evals.py, parallel workers)
- The agent needs API access (network) from inside the container for model calls
- File changes must persist and be extractable as a git diff

EXISTING CODE TO LEVERAGE:
- evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py — already has Docker image building logic for scoring
- The `swebench` Python package handles image lifecycle
- Our verify_after_execute step already runs tests in Docker

START BY:
1. Understanding how SWE-bench builds and runs Docker containers (read swebench.harness code)
2. Understanding how our verify step works (evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py verify_after_execute)
3. Prototyping the simplest approach that works
4. Testing with ONE task end-to-end

This is a significant infrastructure change. Get it working for a single task first, then we'll integrate it into the full pipeline.

        Batch framing:
        - Execute batch 2 of 4.
        - Actionable task IDs for this batch: ['T3', 'T4']
        - Already completed task IDs available as dependency context: ['T1', 'T2']

      "description": "Implement megaplan_env_overrides(), prompt modification, and patch extraction.\n\n### A. megaplan_env_overrides (swe_bench.py:588-589)\nReplace the empty return:\n```python\ndef megaplan_env_overrides(self, prepared) -> dict[str, str]:\n    image = prepared.metadata.get('docker_image', '')\n    if not image:\n        return {}\n    return {\n        'TERMINAL_ENV': 'docker',\n        'TERMINAL_DOCKER_IMAGE': image,\n        'TERMINAL_CWD': '/testbed',\n        'TERMINAL_DOCKER_SKIP_HOME_OVERLAY': 'true',\n        'TERMINAL_DOCKER_SKIP_SECURITY_ARGS': 'true',\n        'TERMINAL_DOCKER_FORWARD_ENV': json.dumps(_get_provider_env_var_names()),\n        'TERMINAL_CONTAINER_PERSISTENT': 'false',\n        'TERMINAL_CONTAINER_MEMORY': '16384',\n        'TERMINAL_DOCKER_EXTRA_ARGS': json.dumps([\n            '--pids-limit', '4096',\n            '--tmpfs', '/tmp:rw,nosuid,size=4g',\n            '--tmpfs', '/var/tmp:rw,noexec,nosuid,size=1g',\n            '--tmpfs', '/run:rw,noexec,nosuid,size=256m',\n        ]),\n        'TERMINAL_DOCKER_VOLUMES': json.dumps([f'{prepared.path}:/hermes_output']),\n    }\n```\nKey: mount the host workspace at /hermes_output (NOT /testbed). Agent works in /testbed (container's own repo). Writes diff to /hermes_output.\n\n### B. _get_provider_env_var_names() helper\nAdd module-level function:\n```python\ndef _get_provider_env_var_names() -> list[str]:\n    names = {'OPENROUTER_API_KEY', 'OPENAI_API_KEY', 'ANTHROPIC_API_KEY',\n             'ANTHROPIC_TOKEN', 'OPENAI_BASE_URL', 'HERMES_MODEL'}\n    try:\n        from hermes_cli.auth import PROVIDER_REGISTRY\n        for pconfig in PROVIDER_REGISTRY.values():\n            if pconfig.auth_type == 'api_key':\n                names.update(pconfig.api_key_env_vars)\n    except ImportError:\n        pass\n    return sorted(names)\n```\n\n### C. Prompt modification (read_prompt, swe_bench.py:275-328)\nAfter the existing instructions section (~line 326), conditionally append Docker-specific instructions when `self._docker_execute_image` is set (set by prebuild in T4):\n```\nAfter verifying all tests pass, save your patch for scoring:\n```bash\ncd /testbed && git diff > /hermes_output/model_patch.diff\n```\nThis step is REQUIRED \u2014 without it your changes cannot be scored.\n```\nCheck via `getattr(self, '_docker_execute_image', None)`. Initialize `_docker_execute_image = None` in `__init__`.\n\n### D. Update score() (swe_bench.py:330-424)\nAt the top of score(), after getting workspace_path (line 342), add:\n```python\n# When Docker execution is used, read patch from the file the agent wrote\ndocker_patch_file = workspace_path / 'model_patch.diff'\nif prepared.metadata.get('docker_image') and docker_patch_file.exists():\n    model_patch = docker_patch_file.read_text(encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').strip()\n    if model_patch and not model_patch.endswith('\\n'):\n        model_patch += '\\n'\n    model_patch = _strip_test_files_from_patch(model_patch, instance_id, workspace_path)\n    # Skip the git diff logic below\nelse:\n    # existing git diff logic (lines 350-382)\n```\nRefactor the existing git-diff block into the else branch.\n\n### E. Update verify_after_execute() (swe_bench.py:486-510)\nSame pattern: check `prepared.metadata.get('docker_image')` and read from `model_patch.diff` file instead of running git diff on host workspace.\n\nThis addresses flags issue_hints-1 and patch-extraction-prompt: the prompt instructs the agent to write the file, and score()/verify() deterministically read from it. If the agent doesn't write it, model_patch.diff doesn't exist, and we fall through to the git diff path (which returns empty diff since host workspace is unchanged \u2192 scored as failed \u2192 triggers retry via verify loop).",

    "Docker `--tmpfs` args are ADDITIVE, not last-wins. If _SECURITY_ARGS includes `--tmpfs /tmp:...` and extra_run_args also includes `--tmpfs /tmp:...`, Docker may error on duplicate mount points. That's why skip_security_args must fully exclude _SECURITY_ARGS when True, and extra_run_args must provide replacement tmpfs mounts.",
    "The container lifecycle: DockerEnvironment creates a container in __init__ and keeps it alive as self._container_id. But DockerEnvironment lives inside the megaplan SUBPROCESS. When the subprocess exits, the container may be cleaned up (depends on --rm flag). The patch must be written to /hermes_output BEFORE the megaplan subprocess exits.",
    "read_prompt() does NOT receive `prepared` or `config` \u2014 only `task_name` and `source_root`. Docker-aware prompt text must be gated on `self._docker_execute_image` (set by prebuild_docker_image before read_prompt is called). If prebuild is not called (e.g., non-SWE-bench benchmark), this attr is None.",

  "meta_commentary": "**Key design decisions made during finalization:**\n\n1. **skip_security_args instead of extra_run_args override**: The plan proposed appending extra_run_args after _SECURITY_ARGS, but Docker --tmpfs mounts are additive (not last-wins), so duplicate /tmp mounts would error. Added `skip_security_args` flag that completely excludes _SECURITY_ARGS when True, letting extra_run_args provide replacement limits. This addresses flags correctness-2 and docker-sandbox-parity.\n\n2. **Prompt-based patch extraction with deterministic fallback**: The plan's patch extraction depends on the agent writing `git diff > /hermes_output/model_patch.diff`. This is acknowledged as agent-dependent (flag issue_hints-1). Mitigation: if model_patch.diff doesn't exist, score()/verify() fall back to host git diff, which returns empty (no changes on host) \u2192 scored as failed \u2192 triggers verify retry. The agent gets explicit \"REQUIRED\" language in the prompt. This is sufficient for v1.\n\n3. **Prebuild ordering fix**: Moved prebuild_docker_image() call between prepare_workspace() and read_prompt() in run_evals.py. This ensures the Docker image name is available when read_prompt() checks self._docker_execute_image to conditionally add Docker instructions. Addresses flags callers-1 and correctness-3.\n\n4. **Patch-only guard**: prebuild_docker_image() checks both swebench_docker_execute AND swebench_patch_only. Workers with patch_only=true skip prebuild entirely. Addresses flags plan-prebuild-parallel-cost, scope-2, callers-2.\n\n5. **Three container_config sites**: The executor MUST update all three: _get_env_config (primary), terminal_tool.py recreation path, and file_tools.py. Missing any one means a file_read or terminal recreation could create a Docker env without skip_home_overlay, breaking the conda environment.\n\n**Execution tips:**\n- T1 is trivial and unblocks T4's _strip_test_files_from_patch dependency\n- T2 and T3 are the Docker backend foundation \u2014 get these right and the rest is mostly wiring\n- For T4's _get_swebench_docker_image(), test the subprocess call manually first: `python -c \"from swebench.harness.docker_build import get_env_configs_to_build; print('API exists')\"`. If the API doesn't exist, implement fallback immediately.\n- T5 is the largest task \u2014 read the existing score() and verify_after_execute() carefully before modifying. The git diff logic must be preserved as the else-branch for non-Docker mode.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the planned response parsing against the real trace outputs. the final critique messages in the repository commonly contain prose followed by a json object, but the implementation section reduces parsing to case-insensitive substring search over the raw response text. that is weaker than the requested 'try to extract json' behavior and can miscount a keyword that appears outside the actual findings payload. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised parsing approach against the real trace outputs. the repository’s saved critique responses are not plain json strings; they include prose prefixes, and at least one target trace (`django__django-10973`) wraps the json in a fenced code block. a case-insensitive substring scan over the whole response can still credit a model for mentioning a keyword anywhere in wrapper text without proving the keyword appeared in the actual critique findings, so the core comparison logic remains weaker than the requested 'extract json and inspect findings' behavior. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `skip_home_overlay` and `extra_run_args` changes against the actual environment creation paths. the plan only mentions `_get_env_config()` and `_create_environment()`, but both `tools/file_tools.py:109-118` and `tools/terminal_tool.py:960-967` currently build `container_config` dicts that do not carry either of those new settings, so a first `read_file` or `patch` call can still create a docker sandbox with the old `/root` overlay behavior and without the relaxed run args. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `extra_run_args` passthrough against `tools/environments/docker.py:106-116,318`. appending extra args after `_security_args` does not explain how the existing `--tmpfs /tmp...`, `--tmpfs /var/tmp...`, and `--pids-limit 256` entries are removed or overridden, so the revised plan still does not technically solve the parity problem it claims to address. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new patch-file design against current control flow. because `evals/run_evals.py:927-944` reads the prompt before any proposed prebuild hook and `read_prompt()` still takes only `(task_name, source_root)`, the plan's idea of conditionally instructing the agent to emit `/hermes_output/model_patch.diff` is not wired to the actual runtime order and remains technically under-specified. None\n+\n+try:\n+    import docker\n+    from swebench.harness.docker_build import get_env_configs_to_build\n+\n+    client = docker.from_env()\n+    configs = get_env_configs_to_build(client, [payload], namespace=\"\")\n+    if configs:\n+        image_name = next(iter(configs))\n+except Exception:\n+    pass\n+\n+print(json.dumps({\"image_name\": image_name}))\n+\"\"\"\n+        try:\n+            result = subprocess.run(\n+                [sys.executable, \"-c\", script],\n+                input=json.dumps(instance),\n+                capture_output=True,\n+                text=True,\n+                timeout=60,\n+                check=False,\n+            )\n+        except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):\n+            return None\n+\n+        if result.returncode != 0:\n+            return None\n+\n+        output = (result.stdout or \"\").strip()\n+        if not output:\n+            return None\n+\n+        try:\n+            payload = json.loads(output.splitlines()[-1])\n+        except json.JSONDecodeError:\n+            return None\n+\n+        image_name = payload.get(\"image_name\")\n+        return image_name if isinstance(image_name, str) and image_name.strip() else None\n+\n+    def prebuild_docker_image(self, prepared, config) -> str | None:\n+        from evals.run_evals import _log\n+\n+        self._docker_execute_image = None\n+        if not getattr(config, \"swebench_docker_execute\", False):\n+            return None\n+        if getattr(config, \"swebench_patch_only\", False):\n+            return None\n+\n+        instance = self._instances.get(prepared.eval_name)\n+        if not instance:\n+            self._load_dataset(getattr(config, \"swebench_dataset\", \"princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Lite\"))\n+            instance = self._instances.get(prepared.eval_name)\n+        if not instance:\n+            return None\n+\n+        image_name = self._get_swebench_docker_image(instance)\n+        if not image_name:\n+            _log(prepared.eval_name, \"SWE-bench Docker image lookup unavailable; using local execute environment\", dim=True)\n+            return None\n+\n+        image_check = subprocess.run(\n+            [\"docker\", \"images\", \"-q\", image_name],\n+            capture_output=True,\n+            text=True,\n+            check=False,\n+        )\n+        image_exists = bool((image_check.stdout or \"\").strip())\n+\n+        if not image_exists:\n+            _log(prepared.eval_name, f\"Prebuilding SWE-bench Docker image {image_name}...\", dim=True)\n+            with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix=\"swebench-prebuild-\") as tmpdir:\n+                predictions_path = Path(tmpdir) / f\"{prepared.eval_name}.jsonl\"\n+                prediction = {\n+                    \"instance_id\": prepared.eval_name,\n+                    \"model_name_or_path\": config.models.get(\"execute\", \"hermes\"),\n+                    \"model_patch\": \"\",\n+                }\n+                predictions_path.write_text(json.dumps(prediction) + \"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+\n+                prebuild_result = subprocess.run(\n+                    [\n+                        \"python\", \"-m\", \"swebench.harness.run_evaluation\",\n+                        \"--predictions_path\", str(predictions_path),\n+                        \"--instance_ids\", prepared.eval_name,\n+                        \"--max_workers\", \"1\",\n+                        \"--run_id\", f\"hermes-prebuild-{prepared.eval_name}\",\n+                        \"--namespace\", \"\",\n+                    ],\n+                    capture_output=True,\n+                    text=True,\n+                    timeout=getattr(config, \"docker_timeout\", 300),\n+                    check=False,\n+                )\n+            if prebuild_result.returncode != 0:\n+                _log(prepared.eval_name, \"SWE-bench Docker prebuild failed; using local execute environment\", dim=True)\n+                return None\n+\n+        prepared.metadata[\"docker_image\"] = image_name\n+        self._docker_execute_image = image_name\n+        return image_name\n \n     def read_prompt(self, task_name: str, source_root: str | Path) -> str:\n         instance = self._instances.get(task_name)\n*** End Patch\n"
}

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Run the Hermes agent inside the SWE-bench Docker container for code execution and testing.

PROBLEM:
Currently the Hermes agent runs on macOS (Python 3.11, warm imports), writes code, and tests it locally. Then SWE-bench scores in a Docker container (Python 3.9, clean state). Environment differences cause false passes: tests pass locally but fail in Docker. This accounts for multiple failures across our eval runs.

SOLUTION:
Make the executor phase run inside the same Docker container that SWE-bench uses for scoring. The agent reads files, edits code, runs tests — all inside the container. When tests pass in this environment, they'll pass in scoring too.

HOW SWE-BENCH DOCKER WORKS:
- SWE-bench builds Docker images per-repo: `sweb.env.py.x86_64.<hash>:latest`
- These images have the repo pre-installed at the right commit with all dependencies
- Tests run via `pytest` or the repo's test runner
- The `swebench.harness` module handles image building and test execution

WHAT WE NEED:
1. Before execute phase, build or pull the SWE-bench Docker image for the current task
2. Mount the workspace (with the code changes) into the container
3. Run the Hermes agent with the execute prompt, but with tool calls (terminal, file operations) executing INSIDE the container
4. The agent can read/write files in the mounted workspace and run tests in the container's environment
5. After execution, the patch is extracted from the container and scored normally

APPROACH OPTIONS (investigate and choose the best):

A. **Docker exec approach**: Build the SWE-bench image, start a container, mount the workspace. The hermes terminal tool runs commands via `docker exec` instead of local shell. File tools work on the mounted volume.

B. **Remote execution approach**: Start the container with an SSH server or API endpoint. Hermes connects to it for terminal commands. 

C. **Full agent in container**: Install hermes-agent inside the Docker container. Run the entire execute phase inside. Pass the prompt and API keys via env vars.

D. **Hybrid**: Run hermes locally but redirect ONLY test commands to Docker via a wrapper script.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must use the EXACT same Docker image SWE-bench uses for scoring
- Must not slow down the pipeline significantly (Docker image building can be cached)
- Must work with our existing eval infrastructure (run_evals.py, parallel workers)
- The agent needs API access (network) from inside the container for model calls
- File changes must persist and be extractable as a git diff

EXISTING CODE TO LEVERAGE:
- evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py — already has Docker image building logic for scoring
- The `swebench` Python package handles image lifecycle
- Our verify_after_execute step already runs tests in Docker

START BY:
1. Understanding how SWE-bench builds and runs Docker containers (read swebench.harness code)
2. Understanding how our verify step works (evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py verify_after_execute)
3. Prototyping the simplest approach that works
4. Testing with ONE task end-to-end

This is a significant infrastructure change. Get it working for a single task first, then we'll integrate it into the full pipeline.

    "Docker `--tmpfs` args are ADDITIVE, not last-wins. If _SECURITY_ARGS includes `--tmpfs /tmp:...` and extra_run_args also includes `--tmpfs /tmp:...`, Docker may error on duplicate mount points. That's why skip_security_args must fully exclude _SECURITY_ARGS when True, and extra_run_args must provide replacement tmpfs mounts.",
    "The container lifecycle: DockerEnvironment creates a container in __init__ and keeps it alive as self._container_id. But DockerEnvironment lives inside the megaplan SUBPROCESS. When the subprocess exits, the container may be cleaned up (depends on --rm flag). The patch must be written to /hermes_output BEFORE the megaplan subprocess exits.",
    "read_prompt() does NOT receive `prepared` or `config` \u2014 only `task_name` and `source_root`. Docker-aware prompt text must be gated on `self._docker_execute_image` (set by prebuild_docker_image before read_prompt is called). If prebuild is not called (e.g., non-SWE-bench benchmark), this attr is None.",

  "meta_commentary": "**Key design decisions made during finalization:**\n\n1. **skip_security_args instead of extra_run_args override**: The plan proposed appending extra_run_args after _SECURITY_ARGS, but Docker --tmpfs mounts are additive (not last-wins), so duplicate /tmp mounts would error. Added `skip_security_args` flag that completely excludes _SECURITY_ARGS when True, letting extra_run_args provide replacement limits. This addresses flags correctness-2 and docker-sandbox-parity.\n\n2. **Prompt-based patch extraction with deterministic fallback**: The plan's patch extraction depends on the agent writing `git diff > /hermes_output/model_patch.diff`. This is acknowledged as agent-dependent (flag issue_hints-1). Mitigation: if model_patch.diff doesn't exist, score()/verify() fall back to host git diff, which returns empty (no changes on host) \u2192 scored as failed \u2192 triggers verify retry. The agent gets explicit \"REQUIRED\" language in the prompt. This is sufficient for v1.\n\n3. **Prebuild ordering fix**: Moved prebuild_docker_image() call between prepare_workspace() and read_prompt() in run_evals.py. This ensures the Docker image name is available when read_prompt() checks self._docker_execute_image to conditionally add Docker instructions. Addresses flags callers-1 and correctness-3.\n\n4. **Patch-only guard**: prebuild_docker_image() checks both swebench_docker_execute AND swebench_patch_only. Workers with patch_only=true skip prebuild entirely. Addresses flags plan-prebuild-parallel-cost, scope-2, callers-2.\n\n5. **Three container_config sites**: The executor MUST update all three: _get_env_config (primary), terminal_tool.py recreation path, and file_tools.py. Missing any one means a file_read or terminal recreation could create a Docker env without skip_home_overlay, breaking the conda environment.\n\n**Execution tips:**\n- T1 is trivial and unblocks T4's _strip_test_files_from_patch dependency\n- T2 and T3 are the Docker backend foundation \u2014 get these right and the rest is mostly wiring\n- For T4's _get_swebench_docker_image(), test the subprocess call manually first: `python -c \"from swebench.harness.docker_build import get_env_configs_to_build; print('API exists')\"`. If the API doesn't exist, implement fallback immediately.\n- T5 is the largest task \u2014 read the existing score() and verify_after_execute() carefully before modifying. The git diff logic must be preserved as the else-branch for non-Docker mode.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the planned response parsing against the real trace outputs. the final critique messages in the repository commonly contain prose followed by a json object, but the implementation section reduces parsing to case-insensitive substring search over the raw response text. that is weaker than the requested 'try to extract json' behavior and can miscount a keyword that appears outside the actual findings payload. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised parsing approach against the real trace outputs. the repository’s saved critique responses are not plain json strings; they include prose prefixes, and at least one target trace (`django__django-10973`) wraps the json in a fenced code block. a case-insensitive substring scan over the whole response can still credit a model for mentioning a keyword anywhere in wrapper text without proving the keyword appeared in the actual critique findings, so the core comparison logic remains weaker than the requested 'extract json and inspect findings' behavior. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `skip_home_overlay` and `extra_run_args` changes against the actual environment creation paths. the plan only mentions `_get_env_config()` and `_create_environment()`, but both `tools/file_tools.py:109-118` and `tools/terminal_tool.py:960-967` currently build `container_config` dicts that do not carry either of those new settings, so a first `read_file` or `patch` call can still create a docker sandbox with the old `/root` overlay behavior and without the relaxed run args. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `extra_run_args` passthrough against `tools/environments/docker.py:106-116,318`. appending extra args after `_security_args` does not explain how the existing `--tmpfs /tmp...`, `--tmpfs /var/tmp...`, and `--pids-limit 256` entries are removed or overridden, so the revised plan still does not technically solve the parity problem it claims to address. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new patch-file design against current control flow. because `evals/run_evals.py:927-944` reads the prompt before any proposed prebuild hook and `read_prompt()` still takes only `(task_name, source_root)`, the plan's idea of conditionally instructing the agent to emit `/hermes_output/model_patch.diff` is not wired to the actual runtime order and remains technically under-specified. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: checked the `must` criterion 'patch is extracted from inside the container via /hermes_output mount and model_patch.diff file'. in the revised plan that behavior still depends on prompting the agent to write the file, so the criterion is not fully verifiable from code or unit tests alone and should be downgraded to `should` unless the implementation adds an automated extraction hook independent of model behavior. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Run the Hermes agent inside the SWE-bench Docker container for code execution and testing.

PROBLEM:
Currently the Hermes agent runs on macOS (Python 3.11, warm imports), writes code, and tests it locally. Then SWE-bench scores in a Docker container (Python 3.9, clean state). Environment differences cause false passes: tests pass locally but fail in Docker. This accounts for multiple failures across our eval runs.

SOLUTION:
Make the executor phase run inside the same Docker container that SWE-bench uses for scoring. The agent reads files, edits code, runs tests — all inside the container. When tests pass in this environment, they'll pass in scoring too.

HOW SWE-BENCH DOCKER WORKS:
- SWE-bench builds Docker images per-repo: `sweb.env.py.x86_64.<hash>:latest`
- These images have the repo pre-installed at the right commit with all dependencies
- Tests run via `pytest` or the repo's test runner
- The `swebench.harness` module handles image building and test execution

WHAT WE NEED:
1. Before execute phase, build or pull the SWE-bench Docker image for the current task
2. Mount the workspace (with the code changes) into the container
3. Run the Hermes agent with the execute prompt, but with tool calls (terminal, file operations) executing INSIDE the container
4. The agent can read/write files in the mounted workspace and run tests in the container's environment
5. After execution, the patch is extracted from the container and scored normally

APPROACH OPTIONS (investigate and choose the best):

A. **Docker exec approach**: Build the SWE-bench image, start a container, mount the workspace. The hermes terminal tool runs commands via `docker exec` instead of local shell. File tools work on the mounted volume.

B. **Remote execution approach**: Start the container with an SSH server or API endpoint. Hermes connects to it for terminal commands. 

C. **Full agent in container**: Install hermes-agent inside the Docker container. Run the entire execute phase inside. Pass the prompt and API keys via env vars.

D. **Hybrid**: Run hermes locally but redirect ONLY test commands to Docker via a wrapper script.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Must use the EXACT same Docker image SWE-bench uses for scoring
- Must not slow down the pipeline significantly (Docker image building can be cached)
- Must work with our existing eval infrastructure (run_evals.py, parallel workers)
- The agent needs API access (network) from inside the container for model calls
- File changes must persist and be extractable as a git diff

EXISTING CODE TO LEVERAGE:
- evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py — already has Docker image building logic for scoring
- The `swebench` Python package handles image lifecycle
- Our verify_after_execute step already runs tests in Docker

START BY:
1. Understanding how SWE-bench builds and runs Docker containers (read swebench.harness code)
2. Understanding how our verify step works (evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py verify_after_execute)
3. Prototyping the simplest approach that works
4. Testing with ONE task end-to-end

This is a significant infrastructure change. Get it working for a single task first, then we'll integrate it into the full pipeline.

  "watch_items": [
    "Docker `--tmpfs` args are ADDITIVE, not last-wins. If _SECURITY_ARGS includes `--tmpfs /tmp:...` and extra_run_args also includes `--tmpfs /tmp:...`, Docker may error on duplicate mount points. That's why skip_security_args must fully exclude _SECURITY_ARGS when True, and extra_run_args must provide replacement tmpfs mounts.",
    "The container lifecycle: DockerEnvironment creates a container in __init__ and keeps it alive as self._container_id. But DockerEnvironment lives inside the megaplan SUBPROCESS. When the subprocess exits, the container may be cleaned up (depends on --rm flag). The patch must be written to /hermes_output BEFORE the megaplan subprocess exits.",
    "read_prompt() does NOT receive `prepared` or `config` \u2014 only `task_name` and `source_root`. Docker-aware prompt text must be gated on `self._docker_execute_image` (set by prebuild_docker_image before read_prompt is called). If prebuild is not called (e.g., non-SWE-bench benchmark), this attr is None.",
    "patch-only workers: `evals/parallel.py:459-463` sets `swebench_patch_only=true` for worker configs. These workers flow through `run_all_evals()`. The `prebuild_docker_image()` method MUST guard on patch_only to avoid wasting time building images that won't be used.",

  "meta_commentary": "**Key design decisions made during finalization:**\n\n1. **skip_security_args instead of extra_run_args override**: The plan proposed appending extra_run_args after _SECURITY_ARGS, but Docker --tmpfs mounts are additive (not last-wins), so duplicate /tmp mounts would error. Added `skip_security_args` flag that completely excludes _SECURITY_ARGS when True, letting extra_run_args provide replacement limits. This addresses flags correctness-2 and docker-sandbox-parity.\n\n2. **Prompt-based patch extraction with deterministic fallback**: The plan's patch extraction depends on the agent writing `git diff > /hermes_output/model_patch.diff`. This is acknowledged as agent-dependent (flag issue_hints-1). Mitigation: if model_patch.diff doesn't exist, score()/verify() fall back to host git diff, which returns empty (no changes on host) \u2192 scored as failed \u2192 triggers verify retry. The agent gets explicit \"REQUIRED\" language in the prompt. This is sufficient for v1.\n\n3. **Prebuild ordering fix**: Moved prebuild_docker_image() call between prepare_workspace() and read_prompt() in run_evals.py. This ensures the Docker image name is available when read_prompt() checks self._docker_execute_image to conditionally add Docker instructions. Addresses flags callers-1 and correctness-3.\n\n4. **Patch-only guard**: prebuild_docker_image() checks both swebench_docker_execute AND swebench_patch_only. Workers with patch_only=true skip prebuild entirely. Addresses flags plan-prebuild-parallel-cost, scope-2, callers-2.\n\n5. **Three container_config sites**: The executor MUST update all three: _get_env_config (primary), terminal_tool.py recreation path, and file_tools.py. Missing any one means a file_read or terminal recreation could create a Docker env without skip_home_overlay, breaking the conda environment.\n\n**Execution tips:**\n- T1 is trivial and unblocks T4's _strip_test_files_from_patch dependency\n- T2 and T3 are the Docker backend foundation \u2014 get these right and the rest is mostly wiring\n- For T4's _get_swebench_docker_image(), test the subprocess call manually first: `python -c \"from swebench.harness.docker_build import get_env_configs_to_build; print('API exists')\"`. If the API doesn't exist, implement fallback immediately.\n- T5 is the largest task \u2014 read the existing score() and verify_after_execute() carefully before modifying. The git diff logic must be preserved as the else-branch for non-Docker mode.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the planned response parsing against the real trace outputs. the final critique messages in the repository commonly contain prose followed by a json object, but the implementation section reduces parsing to case-insensitive substring search over the raw response text. that is weaker than the requested 'try to extract json' behavior and can miscount a keyword that appears outside the actual findings payload. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised parsing approach against the real trace outputs. the repository’s saved critique responses are not plain json strings; they include prose prefixes, and at least one target trace (`django__django-10973`) wraps the json in a fenced code block. a case-insensitive substring scan over the whole response can still credit a model for mentioning a keyword anywhere in wrapper text without proving the keyword appeared in the actual critique findings, so the core comparison logic remains weaker than the requested 'extract json and inspect findings' behavior. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `skip_home_overlay` and `extra_run_args` changes against the actual environment creation paths. the plan only mentions `_get_env_config()` and `_create_environment()`, but both `tools/file_tools.py:109-118` and `tools/terminal_tool.py:960-967` currently build `container_config` dicts that do not carry either of those new settings, so a first `read_file` or `patch` call can still create a docker sandbox with the old `/root` overlay behavior and without the relaxed run args. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
